Anyway Rules: You can make a map with any restriction that you like example: one enemy or only gausses, a no enemy contest or just mines and gold, no jumping, only right. etc. (Nothing like I will make a map in one lifetime).
You also can't do things like no rockets or no gold, if it has a no in it, it has to be something major like not being able to move right (no right movement) or no enemies etc. You will be penalized in points if you do that.

Judges: Evil Bob
Sept
Blazeraid

Back-up Judge: Me

Judging Based on:

Originality of Restriction: /20
Fun level: /10
Originality in general: /10
Tileset and Item Placement: /10
Total: /50


Other Rules:
You can only have two entries.
Maps must be made from 10th of Feb onwards.
No N Art.
Explanations do need to be given.
You will lose points if you take someone elses idea, that is why you can only have two entries.

#7254 1 2006-03-05T10:46:15Z Ultima
Sweep made this for the Contests and Tournaments thread but, It is probably necessary anyway.

1) Are you doing this game just because you want to make a game, or is it because you believe you have a really great idea that would make a really enjoyable game?
Only continue to 2) if you believe the answer is because you have a really great idea that would make a really enjoyable game.
--------------------------
2) Is your the aim for your game clear and simple?
Only continue to 3) if you believe the answer is yes.
--------------------------
3) Has your game been done before in the same or a very similar way?
Only continue to 4) if you believe the answer is no.
--------------------------
4) Have you drawn up a set of rules that are easy to follow and remember?
Only continue to 5) if you believe the answer is yes.
--------------------------
5) Are your game rules logical and as fun as possible?
Only continue to 6) if you believe the answer is yes.
--------------------------6) Have you planned out your game and know what is going to happen. Only continue to 7) if you believe the answer is yes.--------------------------
7) Do you really think people will enjoy playing this game?
Only continue to 8) if you believe the answer is yes.
--------------------------
8) Will you have enough time to maintain the game? Remember that it can be surprisingly time consuming.
Only continue to 9) if you believe the answer is yes.
--------------------------
9) Have you asked someone if they have any suggestions or thoughts for the game?
Only continue to 10) if you have. If you haven't, feel free to ask in this thread so you can get a quality response.
--------------------------
10) Do you have any questions to ask before you start?
If the answer is no, start your contest. If the answer is yes, ask the question(s) in this thread.

Thanks to Sweep for this, I believe this was needed to stem the flow of games that will flop (I have learnt my lesson).

Please pin this.

Ask questions if you need to, Daddaluma or Blue_Tetris will be happy to answer your questions.

Quote: Raigan, March 31, 2006 06:36 pm

if I recall correctly, the way the combo thing works is that each green thing you collect gives you points (based on the current value of the "combo multiplier") AND increases the "combo multiplier" (so that the next green thing is worth more).

however, if a green thing makes it to the top of the screen (they drift to the bottom of the screen, then reflect and drift upwards -- you can tell they're moving upwards because they turn yellow.. I think), the multiplier gets reset to 0 (or 10 or 1 or whatever the starting value is).

so you have to prevent the green things from leaving the top of the screen. genius!

Then I must have had the longest running amount of coincidences ever because, every time I have shot a fully charged blast, my "combo multiplier" went back to ten...

1) Start of round. Task is set to mapmakers (eg, create a map without tiles)
2) Mapmakers create their maps and submit them within a set deadline. Mapmakers must stipulate whether the highscorers must all-gold their map or not.
3) Highscorers have a week to get the lowest overall framecount of all four maps added together.
4) At the end of this week points are given accordingly to the highscorers.
5) Each other nation ranks the maps, and points are given accordingly.
6) Huttah! Match done. If we do this in knock-out form, the team with the highest points progress. In the event of a draw, a vote over which should progress is taken. If this is done in league form, then it's 3 points for a win, 1 for a draw.

How does that sound?

-Atheists don't believe that there is an afterlife, or a God.

-They believe that the world was somehow created. (By that I mean what caused the build up of the super dense matter that caused the Big Bang (assuming that it happened)).

-They have no faith in believing something they cannot see/ has any concrete proof of existing. (It has to be true, or else you would believe in God). Period.

You and your friend are standing on opposite sides of a wall.

(Assume you can hear him shouting through the door).

Quote: Atilla, August 09, 2006 04:09 am
My contention is that the statement "atheists believe they are above God" is at best misleading and at worst totally innaccurate and meaningless.

To keep things clearer, let us focus on the first of these for the moment; that is, I shall begin with an attempt to demonstrate that "atheists believe they are above God" does not give an accurate impression of the beliefs of atheists.

Firstly, if you asked atheists whether they believed they were above God, I'm fairly sure most would say something like "No, I don't believe in God." Furthermore, defenitions of atheism rarely refer to believing you are "above God"; atheism simply refers to the lack of belief in gods, or belief in lack of gods. The statement "atheists believe themselves to be above God", then, is not useful in understanding an atheistic worldview, because it does not describe their core beliefs.

To demonstrate this point further, let us consider other worldviews. If atheists can be said to think themselves above God because they do not believe in Him, then it must be equally true that Christians think themselves above Ganesha (a Hindu god) because they do not believe in him. However, what does the statement "Christians believe they are above Ganesha" tell us about Christian doctrine? Next to nothing. If you were asked to describe the beliefs of Christians, I doubt you would say "Christians believe themselves above Ganesha" at any point, because this is only an incidental consequence. If Hinduism had never existed and there was no concept (note: concept, not the thing itself) of the god Ganesha for Christians to believe themselves above, Christianity would remain unchanged. So it is with atheism - when you say "atheists believe themselves to be above God", you appear to be connecting atheism with the concept of a Christian (or at least monotheistic) god. This is extraneous to atheism; as in the analogy with Christianity and Hinduism, atheism can exist perfectly well without the concept of any specific god.

Finally, the statement "atheists believe they are above God" is misleading because it implies that this is a belief specific to atheism, which is untrue. All non-Christians (or non-Abrahamic religion followers, depending on your intepretation) also fail to believe in your God, and by the same logic must believe themselves above Him - though they may, of course, believe in their own gods.

You know, if you want to get technical, atheists that say:

"I don't believe in God".

Are actually saying:

"I don't believe in something that doesn't exist".

Considering they believe that God doesn't exist.

So saying that they are above God=

"I don't believe in something that doesn't exist but I'm above it, contradicting what I just said and makes me sound like in fact I do believe in God but I am above it. Therefore I am not an atheist even though I am an atheist..."

Which should be what Atilla was trying to argue.


His 2nd last paragraph also owns you.

So, perhaps the box is so small, that even an atom couldn't fit inside of it. Of course, then what would the box be made of? Well, the box never really existed and this entire topic was made by Baraba in conjunction with the rest of the forum to turn it into another Religious debate.
-----------------------------------------------------

Just a question, were you trying to prove a point on Quantum Mechanics or something?

If so:

Quote
Schrdinger's cat
In the world of quantum mechanics, the laws of physics that are familiar from the everyday world no longer work. Instead, events are governed by probabilities. A radioactive atom, for example, might decay, emitting an electron, or it might not. It is possible to set up an experiment in such a way that there is a precise fifty-fifty chance that one of the atoms in a lump of radioactive material will decay in a certain time and that a detector will register the decay if it does happen. Schrdinger, as upset as Einstein about the implications of quantum theory, tried to show the absurdity of these implications by imagining such an experiment set up in a closed room, or box, which also contains a live cat and a phial of poison, so arranged that if the radioactive decay does occur then the poison container is broken and the cat dies. In the everyday world, there is a fifty-fifty chance that the cat will be killed, and without looking inside the box we can say, quite happily, that the cat inside is either dead or alive. But now we encounter the strangeness of the quantum world. According to the theory, neither of the two possibilities open to the radioactive material, and therefore to the cat, has any reality unless it is observed. The atomic decay has neither happened nor not happened, the cat has neither been killed nor not killed, until we look inside the box. Theorists who accept the pure version of quantum mechanics say that the cat exists in some indeterminate state, neither dead nor alive, until an observer looks into the box to see how things are getting on. Nothing is real unless it is observed.


Perhaps not entirely relevant, but interesting nonetheless.

#14409 1 2007-03-12T03:12:47Z Ultima
The Metanet Coliseum!

( If you can't be stuffed reading all of this, all you really need to do is read the bits in the quote boxes.)

This game needs players who come to Metanet at least once every 24 hours.

Quote
Each player starts off by choosing a character and an Element.


Quote
You can pick any character at all, as long as they exist (well, as long as they have a wiki article.)

The Elements work as follows:

Earth > Lightning > Water > Fire > Grass > Earth 

You may pick one Element.

You then pick either Speed, Technique or Power.

Speed means you will be able to use your Power/Super Power more often.

Technique is interesting. Isn't as easy to use as Speed.

Power is the hard hitting choice. It takes a lot of Anger to get the Power/Super Power going, but once you do, insane damage will follow.

You start off with a regular sword, an anger meter with 0 Anger, 30 LP, 0 Gold and the ablility to attack and heal.

Your objective is to kill all of the other players by reducing their LP (Life Points) to 0.

When a player is killed, they become dead and have -10 LP. If a dead player is healed to positive LP, they become the undead and instantly have 10 LP.

If you kill an undead, they are out of the game permanently.


Powers/Super Powers:

The powers (after we choose them for you depending on your character) work like this:

Example:

aaaAAAA

The first three a's represent your power. When you have 3 Anger, you may use the Power.

To use your Super Power, you need to have 7 Anger (in this example) aaaAAAA. Using the Super Power would cost you 7 A.

However, you could use your Power twice, instead and have 1 A over.


Rules/Conditions/Regulations:

Attacking and healing:

You must attack and heal every turn.
-A normal attack does 2 LP of damage.
Healing heals 1 LP of LP.
-Each turn each player may attack one other player. If your element is effective against another element (a water player attacks a fire player etc), you do an extra 1 damage.
-If you heal someone with an Element superior to yours, they are healed by 1 extra LP (applied after the actual heal has resolved).
-If you don't have a weapon (bare fist), you can only every do 1 damage from an attack, regardless of Element.
-You cannot attack a dead player. A dead player may not take any actions.


Anger and Powers:

-The A limit is 20.
-You may use your power and/super power as many times as you want in a turn, provided you have enough A.
-Every turn/round, you are given the ability to attack and heal, buy powerups, use your Power and or Super Power etc.
-You get 1 A per attack against you.

Gold:

-Gold is your currency. You buy things with them.
-Killing a player (not the undead) earns you 30 Gold.
-Killing a player (not the undead) earns you 30 Gold.
-You get 5 gold per round, provided you make an action that round.
-AT THE END of everyturn, the amount of hits you recieve * 5 is the amount of Gold you get that round from the attacks.
-You can sell your items and weapons for some Gold.
-Do 5 damage in a turn, get 5 extra Gold. Do 10 damage, get an extra 10 Gold. The amount of damage you deal in a round (over 5) = the amount of bonus Gold your recieve. The maximum you can recieve through this bonus is 30 Gold. You recieve this bonus the moment you do the damage.

Other:

-If you upgrade a weapon, you lose the previous effects of the weapon.
-The Undead cannot revive players to the state Undead.
-All items can be used on other players (unless specified otherwise.)
-Inactiveness is an extreme sin in this game, you take double the damage from any attack if you are inactive for more than 1 round. (Consecutively.)
-Editing your posts is a no-no. I will go so far as to completely disregard your action if any suspicious editing happens.
-Rules will and can be edited at any time.

Buying and Selling:

Armoury:
The Armoury is only avaliable every 3 turns for 1 turn.

-You can upgrade your sword here.
-Selling your weapon earns you half the amount of Gold that you bought the sword for.
-The sword you start with is worth 10 Gold.

Upgrades:

Mug Shot: For the next 2 turns the normal attack damage you do is also converted into Gold. You get this upgrade onto your weapon by giving in one of weapons (the one being upgraded) for a turn and by paying 10 Gold.
-Ki Power. Sell a weapon for half price to get this upgrade. You Bare Fist attacks do double damage.
-Elemental Sword Upgrades. If your Element is fire, you get a fire sword. If your Element is earth, you get an earth sword. Having this upgrade allows you to do +2 extra damage (instead of 1) against people with an inferior Element. This upgrade also allows you to have 2 Elements in your possession, if you buy an Elemental Force Orb. (You cannot double up an Element.) 20 Gold.
-Twin Swords: This upgrade gives you two swords, and you can attack twice in a turn. (If you upgrade this you'll lose the ability to hold two swords.) 40 Gold.
-Tornado Spear: If the player equipped with this upgrade does more than 3 damage with a normal attack, then that player hit by over 3 damage cannot attack and heal this turn. (If the player has already attacked this turn, then they skip their attack and heal next turn.) 60 Gold.
-Blood Sword: You recover a quarter of the damage you do. (Not just including attacks.) 70 Gold.
-Meta Sword: You gain an Anger point for every attack made when having this sword equipped. 100 Gold.


Power ups/Items:

Elemental Force Items:
-Fire Orb: Changes your element to Fire and adds 1 damage to all of your attacks for 2 turns , it then reverts back to your original element afterwards.
-Water Orb: Changes your element to Water and minuses 1 damage off all attacks against you for 2 turns, it then reverts back to your original element afterwards.
-Lightining Orb: Changes your element to Lightning and you do +3 damage on your normal attack for 1 turn , it then reverts back to your original element afterwards.
-Earth Orb: Changes your element to Earth and everytime you do less than 10 damage (per turn), you gain 2 HP this effect lasts for 2 turns, it then reverts back to your original element afterwards.
-Grass Orb: Changes your element to grass, and take 1HP off any damage done to you, and add it to damage you inflict, +1. For one turn.
All Orbs cost 20 Gold.

Stealing Items: -Drain: Drain an amount of MP from a player equal to the amount of damage you have done from a normal attack this turn. 60 Gold
- Soul stealer: Steal an amount of HP from a player equal to the amount of damage you did to that player from a normal attack this turn. 40 Gold.
-Catharsis: Steal 6 A from a player fro 80 Gold.

Undead Revival:
(All of these items can only be used by the Undead.)
-Zombie Force: Adds 1 attack to your normal attack: 10 Gold.
-Kamikaze Zombie: An Undead sacrifices all of its HP to inflict damage onto 1 enemy. (HP sacrificed - 3 *2 = amount of damage done.) 35 Gold.
-Undead Regeneration: Recover 1 HP. Cost 10 Gold.
-Darkness Claw: The Undead normal attack adds 3 damage onto your normal attack. Cost: 40 Gold.

Healing Items:

- Potion: Recover 2 HP. 10 Gold.
- Super Potion: Recover 10 HP 40 Gold.
-Regen Potion: Recover 2 HP at the start of each turn for 3 turns: 20 Gold.
-Resurrection: Revive a player to the Undead state. 40 Gold.

Anger Increasing Items:

-Anger Pill: Increase your Anger by 2. 30 Gold.
-Rage Tablet: Increase your Anger by 7 for 70 Gold.


Damaging Items:

Interdimensional Rocket: 2 LP damage to 1 player. 30 Gold.
Kamehameha: 6 LP of damage to 1 player. 60 Gold.
Dye-know-might: 1 LP damage to 10 players. 40 Gold.

Support Items:

Indignation Howl: Increase your attack by 1 for 3 turns. 40 Gold.
Healing Staff: Increase your healing ablility by 2 for 3 turns. 40 Gold.

Quote: Atilla, 2007-09-19T07:10:33Z
So, by your logic, killing homeless guys and orphans is perfectly moral because it doesn't make any families angry. Screaming obscenities in a primary school, meanwhile, upsets dozens of families, and is therefore a worse crime than rape.

I didn't say anything was moral. I simply said that one crime was worse than the other. Killing orphans and homeless people is against the law, and will not be tolerated. Also, you're right, my measurement could have been better phrased.

Raping a girl = Police want to catch you.

Killing a bunch of people in a car crash (assuming you're alive) = Police want to catch you more so.

The amount of people being injured or killed is in direct proportion to how much of a criminal you are/how badly the police want to catch you. If they had a choice between catching a serial killer or a rapist, clearly they'd go for the serial killer.
